# Waves

## Properties of Waves

### Transverse and Longitudinal Waves

* **Waves** are disturbances that transfer energy from one place to another.

* Waves can be classified as **transverse** or **longitudinal**, depending on the direction of their vibrations compared with the direction of wave travel.

#|
||

Transverse Waves

|

Longitudinal Waves

||
||

Vibrations are **perpendicular (90°)** to the direction of wave travel.

|

Vibrations are **parallel** to the direction of wave travel.

||
||

Have **crests** and **troughs**.

|

Have **compressions** and **rarefactions**.

||
||

Examples: Light waves and waves travelling along a string.

|

Examples: Sound waves and compression waves travelling along a spring.

||
|#

**Key Terms**

* **Crest:** Highest point of a transverse wave.

* **Trough:** Lowest point of a transverse wave.

* **Compression:** Region where particles are close together.

* **Rarefaction:** Region where particles are further apart.

**Quick Memory Tip**

* **Transverse:** Vibrations are across the direction of travel.

* **Longitudinal:** Vibrations are along the direction of travel.

![](https://znotes-static.s3.ap-southeast-1.amazonaws.com/cie/igcse/physics-0625/66872df5a429782f07a1caa89aade7077e839ba02b342cf0.png =750x)

### Describing Waves

Waves can be described using several important quantities:

* **Amplitude (A):** The maximum displacement of a particle from its equilibrium (rest) position.

* **Wavelength (λ):** The distance between two consecutive points on a wave that are in phase, such as two adjacent crests or compressions.

* **Frequency (f):** The number of complete oscillations passing a point every second, measured in hertz (Hz).

* **Time Period (T):** The time taken for one complete oscillation, measured in seconds (s).

* **Wavefront:** A line joining points on a wave that are vibrating in phase (at the same stage of their oscillation).

**Important Points**

* The **equilibrium position** is the undisturbed position of a vibrating particle.

* One complete oscillation is one full cycle of vibration.

* A frequency of **1 Hz** means one complete oscillation occurs every second.

* Wavelength is measured in **metres (m)**.
